2026-10-25T03:34:42.6296046Z text: [REQUEST] SpeedColumn for arbitrary units (rows, files, it/s)\n\n# Feature Request: Add a Generic SpeedColumn to progress.py\n\n## Summary\n\nAdd a new `SpeedColumn` progress column that displays speed for arbitrary units (iterations, rows, files, etc.), complementing the existing `TransferSpeedColumn` which is specialized for byte transfer speeds.\n\n## Problem / Motivation\n\nCurrently, `TransferSpeedColumn` displays transfer speed in bytes (e.g., `3.2 MB/s`), which is ideal for file downloads or data transfers. However, many progress use cases track non-byte quantities:\n\n- **Rows processed** in a CSV or database\n- **Web requests** completed\n- **Images** or items processed\n- **API calls** made\n- **Generic iterations** over any sequence\n\nThere is no standalone column in `rich/progress.py` that can display speed for these cases. While `TaskProgressColumn` has a `show_speed` option that displays iterations per second when `total` is unknown, it is embedded within the percentage column and uses a fixed "it/s" unit with scientific notation (e.g., `5.0×103 it/s`). Users cannot easily show a dedicated speed column with a custom unit like "rows/s" or "files/s".\n\n## Proposed Solution\n\nAdd a new `ProgressColumn` class called **`SpeedColumn`** in `rich/progress.py` with the following behavior:\n\n### API\n\n```python\nSpeedColumn(unit: str = "it", table_column: Optional[Column] = None)\n```\n\n- **`unit`**: A short string describing the unit (e.g., `"it"`, `"rows"`, `"files"`, `"req"`). Default is `"it"` (short for "iterations").\n\n### Display Format\n\n| Condition | Output Example |\n|-----------|----------------|\n| `task.speed` is `None` | `? it/s` (or `? {unit}/s`) |\n| Speed < 1000 | `42.5 it/s`, `7.3 rows/s` |\n| Speed ≥ 1000 | `1.2k it/s`, `15.3k rows/s` |\n\n- Format: `"{value} {unit}/s"` for normal speeds\n- For speeds ≥ 1000: use `"k"` suffix to keep output compact (e.g., `1.2k it/s` instead of `1200.0 it/s`)\n\n### Example Usage\n\n```python\nfrom rich.progress import Progress, BarColumn, TextColumn, SpeedColumn, TimeRemainingColumn\n\n# Processing rows from a file\nprogress = Progress(\n    TextColumn("[progress.description]{task.description}"),\n    BarColumn(),\n    TextColumn("{task.completed}/{task.total}"),\n    SpeedColumn(unit="rows"),\n    TimeRemainingColumn(),\n)\nwith progress:\n    task_id = progress.add_task("Processing CSV", total=10000)\n    for row in csv_reader:\n        process(row)\n        progress.advance(task_id)\n\n# Web requests\nprogress = Progress(\n    TextColumn("{task.description}"),\n    BarColumn(),\n    SpeedColumn(unit="req"),\n    TimeRemainingColumn(),\n)\n```\n\n## Implementation Notes\n\n- The column should read `task.finished_speed or task.speed` (consistent with `TransferSpeedColumn` and `TaskProgressColumn.render_speed`)\n- Use the `progress.data.speed` style (or equivalent) for consistency with `TransferSpeedColumn`\n- Consider using `filesize.pick_unit_and_suffix` or similar logic for the "k" formatting, or implement a simple formatter (e.g., `f"{speed/1000:.1f}k"` when `speed >= 1000`)\n\n## Testing\n\nAdd tests in `tests/test_progress.py` covering:\n\n1. **Speed is `None`** → `"? it/s"` (or `"? {unit}/s"` for custom unit)\n2. **Small speed** (e.g., 42.5) → `"42.5 it/s"`\n3. **Large speed** (≥ 1000, e.g., 1234.5) → `"1.2k it/s"` (or equivalent)\n4. **Custom unit** (e.g., `unit="rows"`) → `"7.3 rows/s"`, `"? rows/s"`\n\n## Checklist for Contributors\n\n- [ ] Add `SpeedColumn` class to `rich/progress.py`\n- [ ] Export `SpeedColumn` in `rich/progress.py` (if there is an `__all__` or public API)\n- [ ] Add tests in `tests/test_progress.py`\n- [ ] Update `docs/source/progress.rst` to document the new column\n- [ ] Update `CHANGELOG.md` (when submitting PR)\n\n## References\n\n- `TransferSpeedColumn` (lines 694–704 in `rich/progress.py`) - byte-specific speed column\n- `TaskProgressColumn.render_speed` (lines 531–544) - iterations/s with scientific notation\n- [Progress documentation]((rich.readthedocs.io/redacted)\n- [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/Textualize/rich/blob/master/CONTRIBUTING.md)
2026-10-25T03:34:42.6343427Z title: [REQUEST] SpeedColumn for arbitrary units (rows, files, it/s)
2026-10-25T03:34:42.6372829Z body: # Feature Request: Add a Generic SpeedColumn to progress.py\n\n## Summary\n\nAdd a new `SpeedColumn` progress column that displays speed for arbitrary units (iterations, rows, files, etc.), complementing the existing `TransferSpeedColumn` which is specialized for byte transfer speeds.\n\n## Problem / Motivation\n\nCurrently, `TransferSpeedColumn` displays transfer speed in bytes (e.g., `3.2 MB/s`), which is ideal for file downloads or data transfers. However, many progress use cases track non-byte quantities:\n\n- **Rows processed** in a CSV or database\n- **Web requests** completed\n- **Images** or items processed\n- **API calls** made\n- **Generic iterations** over any sequence\n\nThere is no standalone column in `rich/progress.py` that can display speed for these cases. While `TaskProgressColumn` has a `show_speed` option that displays iterations per second when `total` is unknown, it is embedded within the percentage column and uses a fixed "it/s" unit with scientific notation (e.g., `5.0×103 it/s`). Users cannot easily show a dedicated speed column with a custom unit like "rows/s" or "files/s".\n\n## Proposed Solution\n\nAdd a new `ProgressColumn` class called **`SpeedColumn`** in `rich/progress.py` with the following behavior:\n\n### API\n\n```python\nSpeedColumn(unit: str = "it", table_column: Optional[Column] = None)\n```\n\n- **`unit`**: A short string describing the unit (e.g., `"it"`, `"rows"`, `"files"`, `"req"`). Default is `"it"` (short for "iterations").\n\n### Display Format\n\n| Condition | Output Example |\n|-----------|----------------|\n| `task.speed` is `None` | `? it/s` (or `? {unit}/s`) |\n| Speed < 1000 | `42.5 it/s`, `7.3 rows/s` |\n| Speed ≥ 1000 | `1.2k it/s`, `15.3k rows/s` |\n\n- Format: `"{value} {unit}/s"` for normal speeds\n- For speeds ≥ 1000: use `"k"` suffix to keep output compact (e.g., `1.2k it/s` instead of `1200.0 it/s`)\n\n### Example Usage\n\n```python\nfrom rich.progress import Progress, BarColumn, TextColumn, SpeedColumn, TimeRemainingColumn\n\n# Processing rows from a file\nprogress = Progress(\n    TextColumn("[progress.description]{task.description}"),\n    BarColumn(),\n    TextColumn("{task.completed}/{task.total}"),\n    SpeedColumn(unit="rows"),\n    TimeRemainingColumn(),\n)\nwith progress:\n    task_id = progress.add_task("Processing CSV", total=10000)\n    for row in csv_reader:\n        process(row)\n        progress.advance(task_id)\n\n# Web requests\nprogress = Progress(\n    TextColumn("{task.description}"),\n    BarColumn(),\n    SpeedColumn(unit="req"),\n    TimeRemainingColumn(),\n)\n```\n\n## Implementation Notes\n\n- The column should read `task.finished_speed or task.speed` (consistent with `TransferSpeedColumn` and `TaskProgressColumn.render_speed`)\n- Use the `progress.data.speed` style (or equivalent) for consistency with `TransferSpeedColumn`\n- Consider using `filesize.pick_unit_and_suffix` or similar logic for the "k" formatting, or implement a simple formatter (e.g., `f"{speed/1000:.1f}k"` when `speed >= 1000`)\n\n## Testing\n\nAdd tests in `tests/test_progress.py` covering:\n\n1. **Speed is `None`** → `"? it/s"` (or `"? {unit}/s"` for custom unit)\n2. **Small speed** (e.g., 42.5) → `"42.5 it/s"`\n3. **Large speed** (≥ 1000, e.g., 1234.5) → `"1.2k it/s"` (or equivalent)\n4. **Custom unit** (e.g., `unit="rows"`) → `"7.3 rows/s"`, `"? rows/s"`\n\n## Checklist for Contributors\n\n- [ ] Add `SpeedColumn` class to `rich/progress.py`\n- [ ] Export `SpeedColumn` in `rich/progress.py` (if there is an `__all__` or public API)\n- [ ] Add tests in `tests/test_progress.py`\n- [ ] Update `docs/source/progress.rst` to document the new column\n- [ ] Update `CHANGELOG.md` (when submitting PR)\n\n## References\n\n- `TransferSpeedColumn` (lines 694–704 in `rich/progress.py`) - byte-specific speed column\n- `TaskProgressColumn.render_speed` (lines 531–544) - iterations/s with scientific notation\n- [Progress documentation]((rich.readthedocs.io/redacted)\n- [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/Textualize/rich/blob/master/CONTRIBUTING.md)

2026-10-25T03:34:44.4616686Z Stdin is also supported: `safeoutputs <tool_name> .` reads a complete JSON object from stdin. Prefer inline JSON; do not introduce helper tools to construct or submit it.
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4618175Z 
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4619442Z If the sandbox rejects a command, do not repeat it or switch to an unapproved shell workaround. Use a native safe-output function only if explicitly exposed; otherwise call `safeoutputs report_incomplete` with inline JSON containing `reason` and optional `details` when reachable. If even that call is unavailable or denied, state the blocker in your final response; do not probe with real writes.
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4620791Z 
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4621275Z The generated command syntax above is schema-derived from each enabled tool's final `inputSchema` and is the source of truth for required/optional parameters.
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4622259Z Use `<server> --help` and `<server> <tool> --help` for the same schema-derived signatures and examples before calling any command.
2026-10-25T03:34:44.4622796Z </mcp-clis>
